## Universal WebP (m41 + v41, 2026-10-13; owner decision 08 section 17.3): done, deviations, open questions, handover

**Owner's words**: every image uploaded from anywhere is converted automatically (PNG / JPEG -> WebP) to save server space and make images load fast everywhere, stored in the new format and displayed in it. One central pipeline, enforced.

### Done
- **ONE browser pipeline** `src/lib/image` (import from `lib/image/index.js` only; the old `lib/image.js` is gone): `convertToWebp(file, { profile, onProgress, signal })` -> `{ profile, full, thumb, source, report }`. Profiles: `cover` 1920, `landing` 1920 (hero + card covers), `content` 1600 (library images), `avatar` 512 (people photos), `logo` 512 (alpha kept, SVG accepted and kept untouched after a safety check), `frame` 1350 longest side (lossless first, alpha exact, no thumbnail), `template` 4000 / q 0.92 / alpha kept / no thumbnail (**ready for the Certificates builder**), `attachment` 2000 (respondent pictures, no thumbnail). Every profile except frame / template / attachment also writes a 320 px `_t.webp` thumbnail. The format is read from the BYTES (`sniff.js`); EXIF orientation applied (`createImageBitmap(..., { imageOrientation: 'from-image' })`); transparency kept; halving steps for big reductions; quality ladder with a size guard (the first quality that fits `min(target, source size)` wins, else the SMALLEST WebP is kept: it is still WebP, the server refuses the PNG); an already-WebP source that fits the profile is kept as is (no generation loss); `OffscreenCanvas` / `createImageBitmap` when present, buffers released, `AbortSignal` honoured; ONE conversion per (File, profile) (WeakMap cache: ImageField converts at pick time for the preview + feedback, the data layer asks again and gets the cached result).
- **Single door** `data/storage.js`: `storeImage(file, { bucket, path(ext), profile })`, `uploadImage(file, { ownerId, kind })` (kind -> profile), `uploadObject(bucket, path, body, { contentType })` for everything that is NOT an image (an `image/*` type is refused there), `removeImage` (full + thumb), `publicImageUrl / publicThumbUrl` (the only code that knows the `_t` convention: `thumbPath` in `lib/image/paths.js`; an SVG or a legacy raster is its own thumbnail). A refused image becomes `AppError('validation', { messageKey: 'errors.image.<code>' })`: `empty | type | size | corrupt | heic | tooLarge | svg | svgUnsafe | webpUnsupported | aborted` (ar + en). **The original raster is NEVER uploaded as a fallback.**
- **Every call site routed through it** (audited by grep: `.upload(` / `type="file"` / `toBlob`): program cover (create + settings), project / agenda cover, form cover + share image (`og`), landing hero + card covers (`landing`), landing FRAMES (`uploadFramePng` -> `storeImage` profile `frame`), library images (`uploadResourceFile`, private `resources` bucket, `content`) and library resource covers, list item photos / company logos (`EntityForm` -> `list/Edit`, `ParticipantsPage` logo), respondent image answers on public forms (`uploadFormFile` -> `attachment`, private `form-uploads`; non-images go through `uploadObject`). Not an upload (nothing stored, left as it is): the visitor's composed photo of the Landing "Personalize" section (a PNG DOWNLOAD built from the visitor's own photo), the certificate PNG / PDF export. There is no avatar upload in the app (profiles only have a colour).
- **Enforcement in code**: ESLint `no-restricted-syntax` flags `.upload(` / `.uploadToSignedUrl(` outside `data/storage.js` (proved with a throwaway file: lint error), `data/storage.guard.test.js` scans `src` for direct uploads, raw `/storage/v1/object` calls, raster content types handed to storage and stray canvas encoders. The one allowed exception is `data/certificates.js` (the Certificates builder's call site, see handover).
- **Display** (`ui/Img`, `ui/Avatar`, every list / card / editor thumbnail): lists and cards load the `_t.webp`, detail views the full file; a reserved `aspect` box (no layout shift); `loading="lazy"` + `decoding="async"` everywhere except THE above-the-fold cover / hero (`priority` = `loading="eager"` + `fetchpriority="high"`: the program page cover, the landing hero); a development warning in `Img` when a storage URL still ends in .png / .jpg / .gif. Confirmed in the browser: the programs list requested `..._t.webp` (lazy), the program page `...webp` (eager, `fetchpriority=high`), the landing hero eager + high.
- **Feedback** (`ui/ImageField`, the form cover toast, the Library upload toasts, the frame toast, the public form's file field): "Reading / Converting to WebP / Preparing the thumbnail / Uploading" with a progress bar, then "Optimised: 2.1 MB -> 180 KB (-91%)" (never "-100%": the percentage is capped at 99 while bytes remain), or "Already WebP, kept as is", "SVG kept as is", "Converted to WebP: 120 KB -> 140 KB", the animated-GIF notice, "Resized to 1920 x 1440 px".
- **Server** `db/migrations/m41_webp.sql` + `db/verify/v41_webp.sql` (47 checks): bucket lists, two RESTRICTIVE storage policies, `nonwebp_image_objects`, `storage_dangling_refs()`, `rewrite_storage_refs()`. **Converter** `db/scripts/convert-images.mjs` + `run-convert-images.sh` (sharp in a scratch dir OUTSIDE the repo, dry run by default, never deletes). **Edge function skeleton** `supabase-new/functions/image-convert/` (+ `webhook.sql`, README; not deployed, codec not wired). Details and every number in `db/MIGRATION-LOG.md` (m41) and `web/README.md` ("Universal WebP").
- **Static brand pattern**: `public/brand/pattern.png` (442 KB, the login / 404 / no-access background) is now `pattern.webp` (204 KB, lossless, verified pixel-identical wherever visible); `tailwind.config.js` (`hero-pattern`) and `lib/brand.js` point at it; the original stays in `design-system/raw/images/`. The logos (1 - 6 KB) and the favicon stay PNG (not worth a variant; a favicon should be PNG / ICO).

### Verification (local stack, dev server port 5402, own tab)
- `npm run lint` clean (the only warning is in another builder's `w40-harness.jsx`); `npx vitest run`: **65 files, 670 tests, all green** (new / changed for this work: `lib/image/*.test.js` 54 tests with a fake canvas backend, `data/storage.test.js`, `data/storage.guard.test.js`, `ui/ImageField.test.jsx`); `npm run build` green: the whole pipeline is ONE lazy chunk `storage-*.js` (11.7 kB / 4.8 kB gzip), not in the entry chunk (11.6 kB).
- **A real-browser bug found and fixed**: the WebP-support probe drew nothing on its `OffscreenCanvas` and `convertToBlob` throws `InvalidStateError` on a canvas with no rendering context, so the probe said "this browser cannot convert images to WebP" in current Chrome (unit tests with a fake canvas could not see it). The probe now gets a 2d context and paints a pixel; the fake canvas of `canvas.test.js` throws the same error without one.
- **Browser walk-through** (throwaway program / project / user, all removed): each upload below was converted in the browser, stored as `image/webp`, and the DB path column / config ends in `.webp` (checked in `storage.objects` and in the row):

| Where | Source | Stored (full + thumb) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Program cover (Settings) | PNG 3000 x 2000, 16.3 MB | 67,244 B + 2,956 B | -99.6% (a gradient: best case) |
| Project / Agenda cover | JPEG 4000 x 3000, 3.8 MB | 37,810 B + 3,274 B | resized to 1920 x 1440 |
| List item photo (expert) | JPEG with EXIF orientation 6, 3000 x 2000 | 1,008 B + 782 B | stored upright (341 x 512): EXIF respected |
| Library image | JPEG 2000 x 1500 pure noise, 2.4 MB | 675,404 B + 8,100 B | worst case: nothing beats the 0.5 floor; still -72% |
| Landing hero | PNG 1200 x 630, 1.9 MB | 14,218 B + 2,454 B | |
| Landing card cover | the same noise JPEG, 2.4 MB | 1,005,558 B + 8,494 B | -58% on noise (profile floor 0.55) |
| Landing FRAME | PNG 2160 x 2700 with alpha, 78 KB | 17,456 B (no thumb) | 1080 x 1350, **alpha exact**: 57.4% transparent / 42.6% opaque / 0 partial before AND after |
| Form share image (`og`, Settings) | JPEG 4000 x 3000, 3.8 MB | 37,810 B + 3,274 B | the path lands in the node config |
| Form cover (cover library picker, `form-cover`) | JPEG 4000 x 3000, 3.8 MB | 37,810 B + 3,274 B | `form_covers.image_path` ends in `.webp` (a second, identical throwaway fixture; also removed) |
| Public form image answer (anon, private `form-uploads`) | PNG 16.3 MB; then PNG 1.9 MB | 79,548 B; 12,376 B | the answer stores the `.webp` path; the submit succeeded |
| Company logo (list item) | PNG 800 x 400 with alpha, 10.3 KB | 6,340 B + 3,650 B | alpha 68.4% -> 68.9% (kept); SVG 203 B stored untouched (no thumb) |

  The 13 source files totalled 52.8 MB; their full WebP outputs total about 1.99 MB (about -96%), plus about 35 KB of thumbnails (two of the files are pure-noise worst cases that account for 1.7 MB of the output). The one-shot `frame` upload shows "Frame uploaded. Optimised: 22 KB -> 17 KB (-23%)".
- **Failure cases** (clear message in the field, nothing uploaded): truncated JPEG / PNG -> "This image is damaged or incomplete and cannot be read" (ar: "هذه الصورة تالفة أو ناقصة ..."); a text file named `.png` and a `.pdf` -> "Only PNG, JPEG, WebP or GIF images are accepted"; a HEIC -> "HEIC/HEIF photos cannot be read by this browser. Export the photo as JPEG or PNG"; an SVG in a non-logo field -> not accepted; an animated GIF -> first frame kept + "This image is animated: only its first frame was kept"; on the public form a `.heic` is refused by the extension list ("file type not accepted").
- **Direct Storage API**: PNG / JPEG / GIF into `public-assets`, `founder-assets`, `resources` = HTTP 400 `invalid_mime_type`; the same PNG relabelled `image/webp` under a `.png` name = HTTP 400 (policy); WebP = 200; SVG into `public-assets` = 200 (logos), into `founder-assets` = 400; PDF into `resources` unchanged = 200.
- **Rendering**: Arabic RTL and English LTR at desktop width and at 375 px (Arabic landing view and Arabic ImageField at 375: no horizontal scroll, the feedback lines wrap inside the field).
- **Dry run + apply of the converter**: see `db/MIGRATION-LOG.md` (m41): 153 raster originals (87.1 MB; 83.3 MB -> 5.8 MB measurable) in `event-assets` / `migration-archive`, **0 referenced by current data**, so `--apply` has nothing to convert on this DB; the apply path was proved on a simulated legacy data set (4 references rewritten, `storage_dangling_refs()` = 0, originals untouched, then removed).
- **Baseline after cleanup**: 12 / 12 / 12 users / profiles / roles, 4 programs, 16 projects, 82 nodes, 53 edges, 141 companies, 44 program_members, 0 participants, 0 certificates, 612 list_items, 384 responses, 55 slots, 30 bookings, 716 sessions, 373 storage objects, 82 node_revisions, 0 staff_invitations: **no delta** (only throwaway data was created and every row and object was removed; an orphan scan for the throwaway ids found nothing). One intentional change outside the DB: `public/brand/pattern.png` -> `pattern.webp`.

### Deviations (and why)
- U1. `uploadImage(file, { ownerId, kind })` keeps its signature (the many call sites and mocks stay valid); the profile comes from the `kind` folder (`KIND_PROFILE` in `lib/image/profiles.js`) and a page can override it with `profile`. `ImageField` must be given the same `profile` as the page's upload so the conversion runs once (it is cached per File + profile; a mismatch only means a second conversion).
- U2. Animated GIF / APNG: **the first frame is kept and the result says so** (WebP from a canvas is a still image; covers, photos and logos are not animations and a looping 3 MB GIF is exactly the weight this decision removes). An animated WebP that already fits its profile is kept as it is.
- U3. A WebP that comes out LARGER than the PNG (tiny flat images) is still stored as WebP (the smallest the ladder found): the server refuses the PNG, and "WebP everywhere" is the decision. The feedback says "Converted to WebP: 120 KB -> 140 KB".
- U4. Public form file field: a PICTURE is judged by the pipeline's ceiling (25 MB source, 100 megapixels), not by the 10 MB stored-file limit (a 12 MB phone photo becomes a few hundred KB); documents keep the 10 MB limit. (Found in the browser walk-through: a 16 MB PNG was refused as "larger than 10 MB" before the conversion even ran.)
- U5. `certificate-templates` is NOT switched to WebP-only yet (its call site, `data/certificates.js`, and `lib/certificates` belong to the Certificates builder per the task); the `template` profile and the SQL statement are ready (handover). The ESLint rule and the guard test whitelist `data/certificates.js` until then.
- U6. The Landing frame is still PICKED as a PNG (the editor validates the 4:5 ratio and the transparent window on the PNG) and STORED as WebP; frames saved before m41 are `.png` files and keep working (`thumbPath` leaves a legacy raster alone).
- U7. The visitor's composed photo (Landing "Personalize") stays a PNG download: it is generated in the visitor's browser for sharing on social networks, never stored. Same for the certificate PNG / PDF exports.

### Open questions for the owner
1. **Delete the originals?** `event-assets` (96 rasters, 59.0 MB -> 3.2 MB as WebP) and `migration-archive` (57, 24.1 MB -> 2.3 MB) are not referenced by any data now. Deleting them would free about 83 MB (about 77 MB net after keeping WebP copies). Nothing was deleted; say the word and the converter can write the WebP copies first. 18 of those objects are metadata rows whose file is missing in the storage backend (HTTP 500): they can simply be dropped. **ANSWERED by the owner on 2026-10-14 (decision 18.4): convert all and delete; done in m45, see "Old originals converted + deleted ..." at the end.**
2. **Link-preview crawlers** (LinkedIn, some WhatsApp versions) do not always render a WebP `og:image`: the future share / OG function (docs/04 A-37) may need a JPEG derivative of the share image. **ANSWERED (decision 18.6): the JPEG derivative exists since m45 (`og-images`).**
3. **HEIC** (iPhone photos set to "High Efficiency"): browsers other than Safari cannot decode them; the message asks for JPEG / PNG. A WASM HEIC decoder would add about 1 MB to the page: not added.
4. The brand logos and the favicon stay PNG (1 - 6 KB each): convert too?

### Handover
- **Certificates builder**: route the template upload through `storeImage(file, { bucket: 'certificate-templates', path: (ext) => ..., profile: 'template' })` in `data/certificates.js` (no `.upload(` there any more), then run the statement at the END of `db/migrations/m41_webp.sql` (bucket list = WebP only; add `certificate-templates` to `webp_only_insert` / `webp_only_update`), remove the exception in `eslint.config.js` and `data/storage.guard.test.js`, and re-run `v41`. Templates are rendered at their own pixel size (width x height are stored on the template row; fields are fractions), so keep `template` (4000 px max, alpha kept, no thumbnail).
- **Any new image field**: use `ui/ImageField` (give it `profile`) with `uploadImage(file, { ownerId, kind })`; never call `.upload(` (lint + a test stop you). A new `kind` folder needs one line in `KIND_PROFILE` (and in `db/scripts/convert-images.mjs` `KIND`). Render with `ui/Img` (`variant="thumb"` in lists, `priority` only for THE above-the-fold image) or `publicThumbUrl(path)`.
- **Live project**: run `m41` first, then `db/scripts/run-convert-images.sh` (dry run, then `--apply`), then the `image-convert` function (README there). Nothing here was applied to the live project.
- **Gotchas found**: `OffscreenCanvas.convertToBlob` needs a rendering context (see above); the Browser pane cannot pick files: build a `File` from a local fixture server inside `javascript_tool` and dispatch `change` on the `input[type=file]` (a React file input listens to the bubbled event); a `javascript_tool` click on a page the pane has not drawn still works when `computer` clicks time out; Vite does not pick up a `tailwind.config.js` change (restart the dev server); Storage refuses a direct `delete from storage.objects` (use the Storage API for cleanup); a lazy `<img>` outside a hidden pane never loads (`complete` stays false), so check `src` rather than `naturalWidth` there.

## Certificates per founder (m42 + v42, 2026-10-13; owner decision 08 section 17.2 + 17.3): done, deviations, open questions, handover

**Owner's words**: the name on the certificate is the PERSON's name and the COMPANY's name; when a company has several founders, each founder downloads a certificate from his own interface, issued under his own name (certificates are dynamic on the name of the account owner). Template images only (PNG / JPEG / WebP), no PDF templates.

### Done
- **Model (DB, `db/migrations/m42_certificates_person.sql`)**: `certificate_grants` = the PM's explicit "Issue" action per (node, participant) (company level; attended + project completed; one ACTIVE grant; RLS forced, PM / alumni_manager read only, writes only through RPCs); `certificates` is now the PERSON certificate: `user_id` (the account), `grant_id`, `origin` (`issue | claim | reissue`), snapshot = THAT account's person name + the company name + program / project / dates / template version, a distinct 12-character code, one ACTIVE row per (node, participant, ACCOUNT) (`certificates_active_key` keeps its m38 name). "Linked account" = the founder-platform rule (m39 `my_participation`): `participants.user_id`, or the account's founders row (`founders.auth_user_id`) belongs to `participants.company_id` through `company_founders`.
- **RPCs**: `issue_certificates(node, participants[] | null)` authorises the attended participants AND creates the certificate of every account already linked (idempotent; `null` = all attended, a participant whose grant was revoked is skipped; an explicit selection re-authorises it with NEW codes); result `{ issued (certificates created), authorised, existing, skipped, results[{ participant_id, status, created, linked, unlinked, revoked, no_name }] }`. `claim_my_certificate(node)` (a founder who links / joins after the issue: eligible = linked + ATTENDED + active grant + project COMPLETED + node shown to participants; anything else = `forbidden`; returns `issued | existing | revoked | no_name`; a person revoked on purpose is never re-created). `list_my_certificates(project)` is VOLATILE and CLAIMS first (so `get_founder_program_page`, which already calls it, creates the founder's own certificate when the block opens: no client write call). `revoke_certificate(cert)` = ONE person; `revoke_participant_certificates(node, participant, reason)` = the grant + every active person certificate; `reissue_certificate(cert)` = one revoked person again (needs an active grant, new code). `get_certificates_admin` = participant rows with their FOUNDERS (`issued | revoked | claimable | pending | not_linked | no_name`, counts, no account ids). The public verifier answers `person_name` + `company_name` + program + project + dates (`participant_name` kept as an alias of `person_name`, also in `list_my_certificates`); the legacy `verify_certificate` and `/verify-certificate/:code` are unchanged.
- **Name source of truth (decided, documented)**: the account's founders row in the template language (`name_en` / `name_ar`, the other language as the fallback; the sign-up trigger copies the profile name into it), else `profiles.full_name`; whitespace collapsed; frozen in the snapshot at creation; NEVER a client value and no longer the application's `founder_name` answer. An account with no name at all gets nothing (`no_name`, shown in the panel).
- **RLS**: `certificates_select_pm_alumni_own` tightened (a founder reads only HIS OWN active person certificate while attended), `cert_template_readable` own branch = the certificate's account, `get_certificate_render_payload` owner = the account; anon only through the rate-limited verifier. v38 / v03 notes below.
- **Templates are WebP only**: `certificate-templates` bucket `allowed_mime_types = ['image/webp']` (the PDF type is gone) + two RESTRICTIVE policies `webp_only_cert_insert` / `webp_only_cert_update` (name must end `.webp`, recorded mimetype must be `image/webp`). The upload goes through the universal pipeline: `data/certificates.js uploadTemplateFile` -> `storeImage(file, { bucket, path, profile: 'template' })` (max 4000 px, q 0.92, alpha kept, no thumbnail); `lib/certificates/templateFile.js` accepts PNG / JPEG / WebP up to 25 MB (the pipeline ceiling) and keeps every check (size / resolution / aspect) on the DECODED source pixels; the template row stores the CONVERTED image's width / height. The ESLint exception and the storage-guard whitelist for `data/certificates.js` are removed (the guard test now lists only `data/storage.js`). The editor shows the pipeline's progress and its "Optimised ..." sentence. The renderer needed no change (`loadTemplateImage` = fetch -> `createImageBitmap`, WebP is native).
- **Template variables**: `person_name` (new key; `participant_name` is a legacy ALIAS everywhere: the DB check accepts both, `save_children_certificates` stores `person_name`, an unreferenced legacy template is migrated, a referenced one stays immutable and its payload is emitted as `person_name`, the client maps it in `normalizeField` / `fieldFromRow` / `certValues`) + `company_name` (unchanged key). Editor: both are separate draggable boxes with auto-fit; five samples (long Arabic names, long English names, long person + short company, short person + long company, short names); the default boxes of person / company / program / project no longer overlap on A4 (`program_name` .63, `project_name` .715).
- **Frontend**: Issue panel rewritten (participants -> their founders: Issued / Revoked / Waiting / Not authorised yet / No account linked yet (with the explanation) / No name; per founder Preview, PNG, PDF, Revoke, Issue again; per participant Revoke all; tiles attended / issued / waiting / without an account / revoked; confirm dialogs; Arabic plural forms), `certConfig.js` helpers (`rowState`, `issuable`, `issuableInBulk`, `canReissue`, `canRevokeAll`, `summarize`, `hasCertificates`), `FounderCertificateButton` (person + company + program + code + Preview / PNG / PDF; only HIS OWN), the verifier page (Awarded to + Company), the data layer (`claimMyCertificate`, `reissueCertificate`, `revokeParticipantCertificates`, mutations), error tokens `certificate_grant_revoked` / `certificate_not_linked`, i18n ar / en (251 keys each, parity checked).

### Verification (local stack, dev server port 5403, own tab, throwaway data only)
- `npm run lint` clean; `npx vitest run`: **67 files, 711 tests, all green** (new / changed: `lib/certificates/layout.test.js` + `render.test.js` (**renderer call order with BOTH names: person then company in variable order whatever the field order, each fitted in its own box, long Arabic person AND company as ONE RTL line each, the m38 alias**), `templateFile.test.js` (WebP accepted, PDF refused, 25 MB), `nodes/certificates/certConfig.test.js` 11 (**per-founder issuance logic**: row states, who can be issued, Issue again only while the participant is authorised, Revoke all, summary counts, draft alias), `nodes/certificates/certificates.test.jsx` 46 (Issue panel with founders, bulk / selected, revoke ONE vs revoke ALL vs Issue again, WebP upload keeps the STORED size, both variables + five samples, alias template, founder block per person, two founders of one company each download only their own, verifier with company), `data/certificates.test.js` 10 (upload through the WebP pipeline into `certificate-templates`, `<project>/<node>/<uuid>.webp`, no thumbnail, PDF / SVG refused before conversion, RPC parameters, claim), `app/pages/founder/founder.test.jsx`, `data/storage.guard.test.js`). `npm run build` green; chunks: certificates `Edit` 37.5 kB (10.9 gzip: both tabs, PM only; was 34.8), `i18n` 41.9 kB (12.1 gzip: the bilingual dictionary, also loaded by the verifier page; was 33.5), `VerifyCertificatePage` 6.6 kB, `CertificateBlock` 2.5 kB, `CertificateView` 2.4 kB, `renderPayload` 4.2 kB, `layout` 7.4 kB; `routes` and the vendors unchanged.
- DB: `m42` applied several times (re-runnable; 0 certificates existed, so the backfill `app_private.cert_backfill_person()` is proved on a legacy-shaped row in v42). `v42` **120/120**; `v38` 98/98 (updated, see deviations), `v41` 47/47 (C03 updated), `v24` 48, `v25` 22, `v30` 23, `v31` 48, `v32` 28, `v33` 62, `v34` 61, `v35` 85, `v36` 73, `v37` 66, `v39` 52, `v40` 107 re-run green. v42 covers: two founders of ONE company get two certificates with two codes and two names (same company name); a non-attended participant gets nothing; issuing before completion is rejected; a not-linked founder / an outsider / an accepted-only founder get `forbidden` and nothing; a founder joining later claims his own certificate on demand (claim RPC, `list_my_certificates` and the Program page RPC), idempotently; revoke one -> only that one (the others keep rendering, the verifier says `revoked` only for it, claim does NOT re-create it); Issue again = new code; revoke the participant = all + the grant, claim forbidden afterwards, "all" skips it, a selection re-authorises with new codes; withdrawn = nothing; a re-opened project / a node not shown = forbidden; a nameless account = `no_name`; RLS (a founder reads only his own row, no grants, anon nothing); the verifier (person + company + program + dates, no ids / e-mail, the render keeps only printed values); the WebP-only bucket (the restrictive policies refuse `.png` / `.jpg` / `.pdf` and a `.webp` that claims a PNG mimetype); the legacy backfill. The local Postgres backend crashed once on an anon call of a function it has no EXECUTE on at the top level of a statement (the known local-image gotcha): that denial is asserted through `has_function_privilege` instead.
- **Browser** (Vite on 5403; a throwaway super_admin + 5 founder accounts created by SQL with a generated password that was served to the page by a local one-shot server, never typed into a tool call; a program + project (Africa/Cairo); the Browser pane is shared, every call carried my own tabId): the PM set the node language English + "show to participants", **uploaded a 2480 x 1754 PNG (173 KB) in the template editor -> stored as `image/webp` (55 KB) in the private bucket, size kept 2480 x 1754**, placed person name, company name, program, date, code and QR (the live preview auto-fit the long English person AND company sample names), ONE Save (v1, `.webp`, 6 fields incl. `person_name` + `company_name`). A company with THREE founders: Sara (account), Omar (account), Lina (no account); a founder of another company whose participant stays `accepted`; a fifth founder not linked yet. **Issue tab -> "Issue to all attended (1)" -> confirm -> "Authorised 1, created 2 certificates"**: Sara and Omar issued, Lina "No account linked yet" with the explanation. **Founder 1 (his own login) saw ONLY his certificate (Sara Ali / CERT42 Nile Labs, own code) and downloaded PNG (457 KB) + PDF (278 KB); founder 2 saw ONLY his (Omar Zaki, same company, a different code) and downloaded his PNG + PDF; the downloaded PNGs were captured from the page and viewed: each prints HIS name + the company + program + date + QR + his code.** The accepted-only founder: page "Accepted ... Nothing to show yet", `claim_my_certificate` = 42501, 0 rows; the not-yet-linked fifth founder: "This program is not available", claim 42501; after the PM linked him to the company he only OPENED the Program page and his own certificate was there (claimed on open, a third distinct code). Anonymous verifier: both codes "Valid certificate" with person name + company + program + cohort + dates and the certificate drawn below. **The PM revoked ONE certificate (Sara) with a reason: only that one flipped to "Revoked" (verifier: Sara revoked, Omar still valid; the panel offered "Issue again" for her).** Arabic: a second node with an Arabic template (uploaded + saved in the Arabic UI) issued 3 certificates; at **375 px in Arabic RTL** the Issue panel, the founder block and the verifier were checked (no horizontal overflow: scrollWidth = 375) and the Arabic certificate was downloaded and viewed (Arabic person name + Arabic company name + Arabic program and date, one RTL line each). Participant level: "Revoke all" -> every founder revoked, the row "Authorisation revoked", "Issue to all attended (0)"; selecting the row and issuing again created three NEW certificates with new codes (the old ones stay revoked). Direct Storage API with the PM token: PNG = 400 `mime type image/png is not supported`, PDF = 400, WebP = 200. Screenshots `design-system/applied/certificates-person-*.jpg` (issue panel en + ar 375, revoke one, the editor with both names, founder 2 block, the late-claim founder, verifier en + ar 375, founder block ar 375, and the three downloaded certificates). One bug found and fixed on the way: the Arabic `issue.done` said "3 شهادة" (number + noun agreement); it now reads label: value.
- **Baseline after cleanup**: 12 / 12 / 12 users / profiles / roles, 4 programs, 16 projects, 82 nodes, 53 edges, 141 companies, 44 program_members, 0 participants, 0 certificates, 0 grants, 0 templates / fields, 612 list_items, 384 responses, 55 slots, 30 bookings, 716 sessions, 373 storage objects (the two template files removed through the Storage API after the certificates), 82 node_revisions, 0 staff_invitations, 3 founders, 0 company_founders, 0 verifier rate-limit rows: **no delta**; an orphan scan over every `project_id` column was clean; the dev server and the two local helper servers were stopped.

### Deviations (and why)
- C1. `participant_name` -> `person_name` (the owner's wording; "participant" is now the company-level row). The old key is an alias in the DB check, the saver, the payloads and the client, so no data is lost and a referenced (immutable) template keeps working.
- C2. The name source is the founders row, then the profile; the response's `founder_name` is not used (it belongs to the application, not to the account that downloads). If the owner prefers the profile first, it is one expression in `app_private.cert_person_name`.
- C3. A separate `certificate_grants` table (participant-level authorisation + revocation) instead of overloading a certificate row: a participant can be authorised with nobody linked yet, and a revoked participant keeps its history. m38 rows are backfilled by `app_private.cert_backfill_person()` (a grant + the participant's own account when it has one; a row with no determinable account keeps `user_id = null`: the PM still sees and can revoke it, no founder reads it).
- C4. "Claim on open" is done SERVER SIDE inside `list_my_certificates` (the call the Program page RPC and the block already make) plus an explicit `claim_my_certificate`; the client has no separate claim step. `list_my_certificates` therefore became volatile (an RPC POST anyway).
- C5. Re-issue rules: "Issue to all" and "Issue to selected" never re-create a PERSON revoked on purpose while the participant is authorised (that is `reissue_certificate`, per founder); a participant revoked AS A WHOLE is re-authorised by selecting it (all linked founders get new codes). m38 treated both as one.
- C6. WebP-only for the template bucket via two NEW restrictive policies, not by editing m41's (m41 stays re-runnable on its own); `v41` C03 ("not switched yet") was updated to the new truth. The bucket's PDF type is gone.
- C7. `v38` changed where the semantics changed (variable key, one certificate per founder account, bulk / selected / re-issue behaviour, panel payload, `.webp` fixture path); each changed check says "m42" in its text. `v03` (stage 1) carries a note: it runs before m38 and the user-level participant of its fixture behaves the same; it cannot run on the current stack since m25 (a pre-existing staff / founder fixture clash), so it was not edited otherwise.
- C8. `issue_certificate(participant, node)` (the m06d name that the stage-1 checks list) still returns a `certificates` row: the FIRST person certificate (an empty row when nobody is linked yet); `issue_certificates_bulk` returns the number of certificates created.
- C9. The default positions of `program_name` / `project_name` moved down a little (.63 / .715) so the four default text boxes do not overlap on A4.

### Open questions for the owner
1. A founder who belongs to two companies of the same project, or to a participant linked both by account and by company, gets one certificate per (participant, account): is that right, or one per account per node?
2. When a founder's printed name is wrong, the PM fixes the founder record and uses Revoke + Issue again (the snapshot is frozen on purpose). Should the PM be able to override a name at issue time?
3. Should a founder be e-mailed when HIS certificate is created (the outbox worker is not built)?
4. A founder who holds certificates from several Certificates nodes (for example one Arabic and one English) sees all of them; the card does not show the node's name yet. Add it?
5. The verifier shows the company name: is that acceptable as public data (the person name was already public)?

### Handover
- **Reuse**: `app_private.cert_is_member(participant, user)` / `cert_accounts(participant)` / `cert_people(participant, lang)` (who is linked to a participant, with the founders of its company, unlinked ones included) for any per-founder feature (notifications, alumni pages); `app_private.cert_person_name(user, lang)` = THE person-name rule; `cert_claim` / `cert_issue_person` = the idempotent "create the caller's own record under a PM authorisation" pattern (advisory lock + a unique-index backstop + a revoked person is never re-created silently).
- **Client**: `claimMyCertificate`, `reissueCertificate`, `revokeParticipantCertificates`, the `certConfig` helpers, `uploadTemplateFile` (the pattern for any private-bucket image: `storeImage` with `bucket` + `path(ext)` + a profile), `PERSON_TONES` / `ROW_TONES`.
- **Alumni pages (step 10)**: `list_my_certificates()` (no project) = all my certificates, already one per account; a manager reads grants through the alumni_manager policy.
- **Gotchas found**: a plpgsql alias named `by` cannot be used as a `record` field (`r.by` fails: pick another name); `jsonb` object keys print by length first, so assert single keys rather than a whole object; the Browser pane captures only the top-left pane-sized area of an emulated viewport (emulate the pane size, 800 x 513, and scroll), and a screenshot right after a state change often times out once and works on a second attempt in a new batch that starts with `tabs_select`; screenshots are saved as files under the session's `tool-results` folder (copy them); `javascript_tool` can `import('/src/data/client.js')` in dev to call RPCs as the signed-in user; capture a page download by wrapping `URL.createObjectURL` and POSTing the blob to a local receiver, then view the PNG; Storage `remove()` of a template file is refused while a certificate references it (delete the certificates first); a bash heredoc that contains certain quote patterns can break the tool call: write the text to a file with the Write tool and append it.


## Review fixes (m43 + v43, 2026-10-13): what changed for every finding, deviations, open questions, handover

Independent reviewers examined the Withdrawn (m40), universal WebP (m41) and per-founder certificates (m42) work. Every medium finding and the cheap low ones were fixed at the root; nothing was patched around. New migration `db/migrations/m43_review_fixes.sql` (re-runnable, patches the CURRENT function definitions in place, m40 / m42 untouched) + `db/verify/v43_review_fixes.sql` (103 checks).

### Findings and what was changed

| # | Finding | What was changed |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `request_withdrawal` three-valued-logic hole (any user could flag any company-level participant) | Re-created null-safe (every operand `coalesce`d, `uid is null` refused, the row read without a lock before the check). **Audit of every guard of the catalogue** (script over all 251 functions): the same NULL trap was also in `cert_is_member`, the certificate render payload and the re-issue guard (below) and in 12 `auth.role()` guards (`<>` -> `is distinct from`, `= 'service_role'` -> `coalesce(.., false)`; through PostgREST the claim is always set, so this is hardening, not an exploit). v43 A01-A09, C04, C05. |
| 2 | `get_certificate_render_payload` leaked the full payload when `certificates.user_id` is NULL | `if not coalesce(v_own, false)`; `cert_is_member` returns `coalesce(.., false)` and never NULL. v43 B01-B06. |
| 3 | A withdrawn EXPERT stayed bookable / listed through a chained copy | One fact, written everywhere: `set_item_withdrawn` (re-created on top of `app_private.withdraw_item_core`) writes the flag on EVERY `list_items` row of the same expert IN THAT PROJECT (not other projects, not manual items), a BEFORE INSERT trigger copies it to a copy materialised later, reinstating from ANY copy clears them all; an advisory lock per (project, expert) serialises a withdraw with a materialisation (one lock order, no deadlock with the row locks). The one predicate and every consumer are unchanged. Reviewer scenario I00-I08 re-run: item_is_withdrawn(copy) = true, resolver 0 rows, anon booking page empty, `create_booking` = slot_unavailable, distribution payload 0 mentors, presenter hidden. v43 D01-D14. |
| 4 | E-mail-only linking trusted `email_confirmed_at`; with "Confirm email" OFF anyone can sign up with an applicant's address and get a verifiable certificate under the victim company | **Evidence**: all 12 migrated accounts (a copy of LIVE) show the auto-confirm signature (`confirmation_sent_at` NULL, confirmed within milliseconds of creation), so on the live project the setting is probably OFF today: the finding is real, not conditional. Fix: `app_private.auth_email_verified(user)` = a confirmation that was SENT and then CLICKED (`confirmation_sent_at is not null and email_confirmed_at >= confirmation_sent_at`); `link_my_participations` (m39) uses it. With confirmations OFF nothing links by e-mail (fail-safe); with ON it works unchanged. Proved in the browser (sign-up with an accepted applicant's e-mail: 0 programs; after the confirmation click is simulated: linked). A founder linked by PM-curated `company_founders` / the invitation token is unaffected. **Still the owner's to do before go-live: turn "Confirm email" ON (decision 17.4).** v43 G01-G07. |

Measured in the browser: chip 5.25:1, reason 5.37:1, name 5.37:1; the Participants row (reason 5.37:1, chip 5.25:1) follows the same rule, its links are no longer under any opacity. Unit test asserts no `opacity-` on the row or on the chip / reason ancestors. |

The anonymous verifier no longer pulls it (`data/certificates.js` loads storage / the template rules lazily and uses `lib/id.js`). Static-import closure, gzip, HEAD -> now (the review's figure in brackets): AgendaPage 264.3 -> 268.0 (270.7), PublicFormPage 234.1 -> 237.1 (239.8), PublicLandingPage 236.8 -> 239.6 (242.4), FounderProgramsPage 226.9 -> 229.9 (232.6), FounderProgramPage 232.5 -> 235.5 (238.2), ListTab 227.6 -> 231.8 (234.3), **VerifyCertificatePage 228.5 -> 231.3 (237.7), no storage chunk**; the rest is the 3.0 KB storage chunk (URL helpers + the upload entry points) and the certificates i18n. |

Lossless for the frames (quality 1), alpha kept, null on any failure (the original is never uploaded). Proved in the browser with the development switch `window.__forceNoNativeWebp = true`: a 3000 x 2000 PNG (5.8 MB) -> a real VP8 WebP (RIFF / WEBP / "VP8 ") 1920 x 1280, 24 KB, full + thumbnail stored; a 2480 x 1754 certificate template -> 27.7 KB, decoded back at 2480 x 1754. The native path (switch off) never requests a .wasm. **Not run on a real Safari / iPhone (no device here).** 9 unit tests (`wasmWebp.test.js`, `canvas.test.js`). The "unsupported" message now only appears when the fallback itself cannot load. |
